## Decisions log

Rulings made during the loop that future tasks must respect. A decision big enough to
constrain a whole module belongs in `docs/14-Engineering/ADR/` instead — record the ADR
number here and move on.

| ID | Date | Task | Decision | Rationale |
| -- | ---- | ---- | -------- | --------- |
| `DEC-L-001` | 2026-08-25 | — | `SSAS_ERP_V2_TENANT_ARCH/` is **read-only reference and never an authority**. It is an Aug-13 snapshot holding ADR-001…021 only. | The main repo carries ADR-001…027. Sourcing a ruling from the snapshot would silently revert six ADRs. It is configured as an additional working directory, so the hazard is live. |
| `DEC-L-002` | 2026-08-25 | T-001 | A task whose *Files in scope* contains **no file under `src/` or `tests/`** does not run the four-suite gate. Its verification is a `--stat` assertion on which files changed. | The gate proves the build and the suites still pass. Neither can be affected by a change that touches no compiled file, so running it would buy nothing and would teach the loop that the gate is ceremony rather than evidence. Narrow by construction: the moment a `.cs` file is needed the task is `BLOCKED` and comes back to the architect. |
| `DEC-L-003` | 2026-08-25 | T-003 | Roadmap direction after T-001 is the **identity → employee mapping**, ruled by the owner over FP-014 Recruitment and a consolidation pass. | Two delivered packages already stopped at this exact wall: `REQ-ATT-0023` is marked *BLOCKED — NOT IMPLEMENTABLE TODAY* and `OD-PAY-0016` deferred payroll self-service for the same missing input. It is the hard prerequisite for the Self Service roadmap item and it unblocks read paths in two modules that already ship. |
| `DEC-L-004` | 2026-08-25 | T-002 | `CON-0001` is **upheld, not amended**. The product is subscription-based SaaS, and the commercial plane gets a full FP-014 analysis package — chosen by the owner over a gate-only fix and over amending the constraint. | The gap is not that enablement is unenforced; it is that the product has no commercial model at all. A gate built without knowing what it gates would encode a plan shape by accident. Ruling first, code second, which is this repository's established order. |
| `DEC-L-005` | 2026-08-25 | T-002 | The coder's divergence is **upheld**: plan-catalog residency is `DEC-SUB-0003` (settled by `ADR-017`), not an open decision as my spec wrongly directed. Only the genuine residue — where the per-tenant *assignment* lives, and whether entitlement is projected — stays open as `OD-SUB-0004`. | `ADR-017:162` and `:475` rule it twice and explicitly. Hard rule 4: documentation wins over both roles, and that includes over an architect's task spec. Putting a settled question to the owner as open would have invited a ruling against an in-force ADR without disclosing it existed. |
| `DEC-L-006` | 2026-08-25 | T-005 | `OD-SUB-0001` ruled **E + C — the whole plane**. All 28 `REQ-SUB` are in force and no `OD-SUB` collapses. | Owner ruling over enablement-only and commerce-only. `CON-0001` says *subscription-based SaaS*, not feature flags; E alone would gate modules the product cannot sell, and E-then-C would touch the same tenant record twice. Cost accepted: five commercial decisions with no defaults, several regulated, block the build prompt until ruled. |
| `DEC-L-007` | 2026-08-25 | — | **The loop merges its own work.** A task with a file under `src/` or `tests/` in scope merges itself via `gh pr create` + `gh pr merge --merge` the moment every gate suite has run and passed — no wait on the architect, who reviews afterwards. A task with neither pushes and waits for `MERGE T-###`. Direct pushes to `main` stay denied; every merge leaves a PR. | Owner ruling. The gate is 2,681 tests including 408 architecture guards — a real bar, and fast feedback is worth more than a review round-trip on code it can judge. It cannot judge a document: `DEC-L-002` waives the gate where nothing compiles, so for those the architect's verification is the only check that exists, and every non-gated task so far has returned a real finding. |
| `DEC-L-008` | 2026-08-25 | — | **Green means tests pass, not that the build succeeded.** Four conditions, all required before a merge: zero-warning build; every gate suite ran this session and passed; `Integration.Tests` too where the task touched persistence, a migration, an EF configuration or the cutover inventory; **and the tests the task required exist in this diff and pass.** Counts reported before and after, against the recorded baseline. | Owner tightening. Condition 4 is the substantive one: the first three prove the existing suite still passes, which a task that adds untested code satisfies trivially. A suite that is green because nothing exercises the new code proves that untested code did not break unchanged code — no evidence at all. Under `DEC-L-007` that mistake merges itself, so the bar has to be explicit rather than assumed. |
| `DEC-L-009` | 2026-08-25 | T-008 | **Seat cap is enforced at admission, not at login.** Creating or activating a user beyond the cap is refused at that moment, naming the cap and the count. Login is never refused for a seat cap. An excess arriving by plan downgrade is billed, never enforced retroactively. **No grace period, and none is needed.** | The posed pair — block login, or bill overage — is false, and both are wrong here. Blocking a login enforces against an arbitrary user who did nothing, mid-task, in an ERP of record. Refusing at the grant tells the person who caused the excess, immediately, and they can act. Expiry stays the one commercial event that blocks login because it is dated, foreseeable and whole-tenant. |
| `DEC-L-010` | 2026-08-25 | — | **`Platform.` prefix kept; plane made mechanical by an architecture guard covering the WHOLE platform-plane set**, not only FP-014's six new names. | A second scheme for seven permissions is worse than one ambiguous scheme, and renaming assigned permissions buys nothing functional. But guarding only the new names leaves the existing ambiguity — `Platform.Support.*` — unasserted, which is the half that already shipped. |
| `DEC-L-011` | 2026-08-25 | — | **`ADR-017`, `ADR-026`, `ADR-027` move to `Accepted`**, each gaining a line naming the packages and code that evidence it. Content is not reopened. | Five approved packages treat them as binding and four modules inherit `ADR-027`'s `decimal(19,4)`. The front-matter is stale bookkeeping. The alternative reading — four modules of shipped code resting on provisional decisions — is one nobody holds. |
| `DEC-L-012` | 2026-08-25 | — | **The 70% master-register orphan class is closed FORWARDS.** New packages promote their `BR-` rules and cite their constraints at ratification, enforced by `trace-check` check 7; the existing backlog is paid per module when that module is next touched. No big-bang cleanup. | A 51-identifier diff over documents nobody is currently changing is unreviewable and would land unread. Paying it alongside work already in the file is how it survives. `CON-0001` closing via FP-014 is the proof. |

## Open questions the architect owes an answer to

Raised during grounding, not yet ruled. Each needs a decision before it can become a task.

- **`ADR-026` and `ADR-027` both carry `status: Proposed`**, yet `START-HERE.md` and the FP-007/FP-008
  packages treat their rulings as binding, and code has been built on them. Either the ADRs are
  accepted and their front-matter is wrong, or the rulings were applied before acceptance. Needs a ruling.
- ~~**`FP-010`'s README depends on `ADR-028`, which does not exist.**~~ **WITHDRAWN — NOT A DEFECT.**
  `ADR-028` is a **reservation that was honoured**, not a broken reference: `FP-010/decisions-open.md`
  gives it a title (*Binary Content Storage, Custody and Movement*), a full scope analysis preserved so
  V5 inherits rather than repeats it, and an explicit ruling — *“AS OF `OD-DOC-009` (2026-08-23):
  `ADR-028` IS V5'S TO WRITE.”* Sixteen further references across FP-009/010/011 treat it consistently.
  Raised by the coder in T-001, carried by the architect without verification, corrected in T-010.
- **Where the identity → employee link lives** — `Employee` carrying a user identifier, versus a
  Platform-side mapping table. This is ADR-level and blocks T-002.

| T-011   | ADR status reconciliation - 017, 026, 027 to Accepted | Docs | `agent/T-011-adr-status` | `DONE`        | Accepted 2026-08-25. `a234d6b`, 4 files. Coder TESTED the ruling: ADR-026 passed its own stated precondition, ADR-027 had acceptance recorded, ADR-017 is inference and now says so. |
| T-012   | `Authentication.md` takes the supersession-disclaimer shape | Docs | `agent/T-012-authentication-disclaimer` | `DONE`        | Accepted 2026-08-25. `ba9f6d8`, one file. Sweep found FIVE superseding packages, not two. Awaiting MERGE. |
| `DEC-L-013` | 2026-08-25 | — | **The two windows share ONE working tree.** Neither role may verify by reading files from disk while the other holds a branch — verification uses `git show <branch>:<path>` or `git diff <a>...<b>`, never the working tree. Neither runs a build or a suite while the other is mid-task. | Found by the architect: a "baseline without FP-014" run silently included FP-014, because the shared tree was on `agent/T-008-fp-014-matrices`. The comparison was meaningless and looked authoritative. The measured gate baseline survives only because every branch so far has been docs-only, leaving `src/` and `tests/` identical to `main` — that luck ends at T-014. |
| `DEC-L-014` | 2026-08-25 | T-014 | **`gate.sh`'s exit code is not merge authority; green is confirmed by reading the per-suite output** — counts, pass/fail, every leg completed. **Kept in force after T-016 closed both holes**, as a habit rather than a stopgap. | **The rationale this rule was issued on was wrong, and the rule was right anyway.** It was issued believing the abort path exited 0; it did not — that was a `\| tail` pipeline swallowing the status. But two real holes existed at the time: a **red gate** exited 0 (no terminal `exit`, fixed by T-016), and the same pipe that hid the status also buffered 69 minutes of output. Both are closed. It stays because an exit code, however correct, cannot tell you that Architecture moved by exactly three — and reading the per-suite counts is what caught this. |
| `DEC-L-015` | 2026-08-25 | — | **Any task running longer than 20 minutes sends a `PROGRESS` message every 20 minutes** until it finishes: elapsed and estimated remaining with the basis for the estimate, what has completed with real numbers, what is in flight, and **measured evidence the run is alive** — a file timestamp, process CPU, a growing log. Never touch the working tree or disturb the run to produce one. | Owner ruling. A 90-minute silence is indistinguishable from a dead run, and this repository has already produced three independent ways a live run looks finished and a finished one looks alive. Silence was costing the owner a wrong conclusion about whether work was progressing. |
| `DEC-L-016` | 2026-08-25 | T-015 | **An architecture guard must be validated against the defect it exists to prevent: it has to FAIL on the code as it stood before the fix and PASS after.** A guard that passes both ways asserts nothing. Where the property cannot be expressed directly, assert the *surface* that would let it hide rather than weakening the claim. | Generalised from the coder's T-015 reframing. “Every guard sits at the fence” is not expressible — reflection cannot read a method body, so no test can see which method calls a guard. “No context declares a convenience overload at all” is expressible, is strictly stronger, and removes the only place a bypassable rule could hide. It would have failed on `PlatformDbContext` before T-015 and passes after, which is the whole test of a guard. |
| `DEC-L-017` | 2026-08-25 | T-015 | **A silent bypass outranks a loud one.** Where a guard is moved to the fence, any *non-refusing* work on the same path moves with it — and lands **after** every refusal, so a rejected save never leaves the change tracker mutated by a write that did not happen. | `PromoteAssignmentOwners` sat with the seven refusals but is a mutation, not a refusal: bypassing it threw nothing and logged nothing, it simply produced rows whose parent timestamps were never bumped. Of the eight it was the one whose omission nothing would surface, and a fence that catches the seven noisy paths while leaving the silent one behind is partial in the least visible way. |
| `DEC-L-018` | 2026-08-25 | T-010 | **Payment capture is TOKENIZED. No cardholder data enters SSAS — ever.** The product owns invoicing, the payment flow, its state and its reconciliation; the PAN, CVV, cardholder name and expiry are captured by the provider's hosted fields or redirect and never traverse, and are never stored by, any SSAS component. `ADR-001 Modular Monolith` is **not** qualified and needs no exception. | `OD-SUB-0016` ruled the product issues invoices and captures payment itself. Read literally as “the PAN enters the monolith” that would pull every component sharing the process into PCI-DSS assessment scope and force either a carved-out cardholder-data environment — in direct tension with `ADR-001` — or a monolith assessed as one. Tokenization satisfies what the owner ruled (the product owns the commercial relationship and the payment, not a third party) at SAQ-A scope, keeps the modular monolith intact, and is what FP-014 already modelled: its data model states no entity carries a column that could hold a PAN, CVV, cardholder name or expiry, and its API contracts carry the same statement for request bodies, response bodies and logging. This ruling makes that a decision rather than an accident. |
| `DEC-L-019` | 2026-08-25 | T-015 | **Write acceptance criteria that state the PROPERTY, not the mechanism — even when the property may turn out to be unreachable.** A criterion that cannot be met literally forces the question of what *can* be asserted instead; one that can be met literally is met, and the question is never asked. Pair it with `DEC-L-016`: the answer still has to fail on the old code and pass on the new. | The coder's counter-point, and it inverts the obvious lesson. I asked T-015 to assert “every guard sits at the fence”, which reflection cannot express. Being unable to satisfy it produced the surface assertion — no context declares a convenience overload at all — which is strictly stronger than the satisfiable criterion I would otherwise have written. “My criterion was wrong” is the wrong reading; it was unreachable, and that is what made it useful. |
| `DEC-L-020` | 2026-08-25 | T-011 | **An ADR's acceptance is tested against its OWN stated precondition where it has one — not against how widely it is used.** Where it states none, acceptance may be inferred from use, but the inference is **named as an inference** rather than dressed as a record. | `DEC-L-011` argued from use: five packages treat these as binding, four modules inherit `ADR-027`. That argument was sound but weaker than the evidence available. `ADR-026` carried its own test — *“The ADR should not be accepted until `OD-DEP-001`, `OD-DEP-003` and `OD-DEP-005` are answered”* — and all three closed on 2026-08-20. Passing on the document's own terms is stronger than any argument from use, and it is checkable. `ADR-027` had its acceptance **recorded** (`FP-008`: “ADR-027 activates”) and only the front-matter lagged. `ADR-017` has neither, and now says so. |
| `DEC-L-021` | 2026-08-25 | T-020 | **A `Proposed` ADR cannot stand in a defined relationship to an `Accepted` one.** If ADR-X supersedes, amends, or resolves a question deliberately left open by ADR-Y, and Y is `Accepted`, then X being `Proposed` is incoherent in either direction and one of the two records is wrong. A checkable invariant, not a style preference. | Found by the coder in T-020, and it is older than the problem that prompted the task. `ADR-025` supersedes deferred portions of `ADR-014` (**Accepted**); `ADR-024` resolves the question `ADR-023` (**Accepted**) deliberately left open for the first branch-owned entity. Both had been `Proposed` since 2026-08-18. So the `Accepted`-depends-on-`Proposed` inconsistency that `ADR-029` appeared to create in T-011 **already existed one layer down, for a week** — nothing surfaced it because nothing asserted the invariant. |
| `DEC-L-022` | 2026-08-25 | T-017 | **An observed range recorded in documentation must say it is observed, and must say to expect it to be exceeded.** Replacing a wrong range with a tighter one reproduces the defect with better data. Record the samples, name them as samples, and state what the real signal is instead. | The coder's insight, and it is the whole fix. Note 7a's 1,200–1,500 MB band was two samples generalised into a rule; a corrected 550–1,500 band would have made the next low reading look alarming all over again. What closes it is the sentence *treat 550 as the lowest seen so far, not the floor of safe operation, and expect a future run to go lower without that meaning anything* — plus naming the actual signal: **a memory death is no TRX at all**; if the TRX was written and the sampler ran to the end, memory was not the problem whatever it bottomed out at. |
| `DEC-L-023` | 2026-08-25 | T-003 | **The identity→employee link is a Platform-plane mapping keyed by tenant, not a column on either aggregate.** `Employee` gains nothing; `TenantUser` gains nothing. Optional on both sides, at most one live link per user and per employee, **no foreign key in either direction and none possible** — different databases. Recorded as `ADR-030`. | Three reasons. `ADR-017` puts tenant identity/access membership in the Platform database deliberately, and “which employee is this user” is an identity fact. The relationship is **optional on both sides** — most employees have no user; a user may be an external accountant or a support-created admin — and optional-on-both-sides is a relationship, not an attribute. **THIRD REASON CORRECTED BY THE CODER IN T-003:** I wrote that `Employee` is in the cutover inventory *while `TenantUser` is not*, implying the marker excludes it. **`TenantUser` IS `ITenantOwnedEntity`** — verified. `TenantCutoverCopyPlan.Build(IModel model)` filters by that interface **within the model it is handed**, and `TenantUser` is registered in `PlatformDbContext` (9 references) and appears **zero** times in `TenantDbContext`. Cutover membership follows **model residency, not the marker**. The conclusion holds; my stated mechanism was wrong, and a reader checking “is `TenantUser` `ITenantOwnedEntity`” would have got YES and concluded the reason was false. |
| `DEC-L-024` | 2026-08-25 | T-024 | **`ADR-021` stays `Proposed`. FP-014's citations are re-pointed to `ADR-017` instead.** `REQ-SUB-0005`, `DEC-SUB-0004` and the architect's `OD-SUB-0004` ruling move their authority from `ADR-021:207` to `ADR-017`; `ADR-021:207` may remain only where labelled as an implementation-deferred ADR. | `ADR-021` line 37 states its own terms: it moves to Accepted when a customer-hosted deployment is actually contracted. None is. It **fails its own test**, and `DEC-L-020` exists precisely to stop an argument from use overriding a document's own terms — the `ADR-026` shape with the opposite result. | `DEC-L-053` | 2026-08-26 | — | **Code written while a gate runs goes in a second worktree, never the tree under test.** `git worktree add ../SSAS_gate <branch>`. One gate at a time; build sparingly during a run; remove the worktree when the task closes. | The suite reads the working tree, so editing it mid-run makes the result describe a tree that no longer exists - **`DEC-L-013` with a timer on it**. A worktree removes the conflict rather than scheduling around it. The two constraints are real and already encoded: `gate.sh` refuses when a sibling `testhost` from this repo is live and reaps `SSAS_%` catalogs, so concurrent runs would take each other's databases out; and note 7 records that this box *“hosts resident agent sessions alongside the suite”*, so a second `--no-incremental` build can drop it under the 2048 MB LEAN floor and abort the very gate being waited on. |
| `DEC-L-053a` | 2026-08-27 | T-056 | **`DEC-L-053` AMENDED - it named the wrong hazard.** The danger in a second worktree is not memory. `reap_to_zero` drops every `SSAS[_]%` catalog under **every** scope including `TASK`, and the sibling-`testhost` precondition matches on `basename "$ROOT"` - so two differently-named trees each judge the other's testhost to be someone else's and proceed. **A 72-second `TASK` gate destroys a 69-minute `PHASE` run's catalogs mid-leg, silently, on both sides.** The shared resource is the SQL Server instance, not the repository. Until T-056 lands this is kept by hand and nothing warns you. | **The architect wrote the rule and then issued an instruction that would have executed the failure** - *“start the clean `PHASE` run now so it is doing something while you do the cheap checks”*. The coder refused it and supplied the mechanism. **And the error compounds across the two rules shipped in the same commit:** `DEC-L-051` made a per-task gate cost 72 seconds, and something that cheap does not feel like an action needing a check first. **The hazardous action was made attractive in one rule and mislabelled in the next.** A rule that enumerates the wrong constraints is worse than none, because it reads as though the hazards were listed. |
| `DEC-L-054` | 2026-08-27 | T-055 | **A `git` result is not a statement about the working tree. Name the reference before acting on it.** `git status` reports against **HEAD**; `git diff <commit> -- <path>` reports against the **INDEX**; neither names its reference in its output, and both render as though they described the working tree. **When the question is “does this file's CONTENT match `X`”, the answer is `git hash-object <path>` against `git rev-parse X:<path>`** - the one form that names both sides. | **Root cause under two instances in one hour, found by the coder, and worth more than either.** The coder read ` M` in `git status` as *“uncommitted work”* when on a branch predating the merge it means **the opposite** - already merged, HEAD is behind. The architect read `git diff origin/main -- <path>` as *“differs”* when for an untracked path it means **the index is empty**. Both commands returned correct answers about references neither had named. **Not carelessness: the display is identical for opposite facts.** |
| `DEC-L-054a` | 2026-08-27 | T-055 | **`DEC-L-054`'s mechanism AMENDED with why it works, because the obvious substitute is wrong here.** `git hash-object` **normalises CRLF→LF through the clean filter, then prefixes `blob <size>\0`** before hashing. **Proven, not assumed:** `sha1(header + LF-normalised)` == `git hash-object` == `5756b8e4…`, while `sha1(header + raw CRLF bytes)` = `a18d1726…` and bare `sha1sum` = `eda9967c…`. **So `sha1sum`, `Get-FileHash` and `certutil` are wrong here for TWO independent reasons - the header AND the normalisation - and either alone breaks the comparison.** `core.autocrlf=true`; `CODER.md` holds 356 CR and 356 LF. | Raised and tested by the coder **before relying on the rule**, on a file both sides had already established identical - so a mismatch would have indicted the instrument rather than the file, which is `DEC-L-046`'s ordering applied correctly. **The architect doubted both halves of the claim and was wrong on both:** it hypothesised the blob header alone explained the mismatch (it does not - the header-only hash still differs) and probed the file as LF (it is CRLF). |
| `DEC-L-054b` | 2026-08-27 | T-055 | **`DEC-L-054`'s MECHANISM IS REPLACED, NOT PATCHED. Use `git ls-tree <ref> -- <path>`, which contains no colon and needs no environment variable at all.** The comparison is `git hash-object <path>` against `git ls-tree <ref> -- <path> \| awk '{print $3}'`. **The old `git rev-parse <ref>:<path>` form fails on every dot-prefixed path in Git Bash** - `.claude/`, `.github/`, `.gitignore` all mangle to `origin\main;.claude\roles\CODER.md` - **and every handoff file this loop uses lives under `.claude/`.** | **The coder found that `DEC-L-054`'s mechanism was load-bearing on `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV`, which `DEC-L-056` had just restricted** - the two rules issued hours apart were in direct conflict, and it verified the mechanism at 10:48 with the variable exported without noticing the dependency. **Rather than document the dependency, it was removed.** Tested with the variable UNSET across **eight paths**: `.claude/roles/CODER.md`, `.claude/handoff/BOARD.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.gitignore` all fail under `rev:path` and all succeed under `ls-tree`; `src/`, `docs/`, `scripts/`, `README.md` succeed under both. **The dot-prefix regularity is TESTED across eight cases and the MSYS conversion rule behind it is still NOT asserted** - the coder declined to guess it and the architect is not guessing either. |
| `DEC-L-055` | 2026-08-27 | — | **THE FINDINGS REGISTER IS NOT A LEDGER. An entry earns its place by being FOUND, never by being CONTRIBUTED to balance a count.** Neither window adds a finding against itself to keep the tally level, and neither softens one to avoid a lopsided day. **A day that ends lopsided in either direction is information about the day, not a debt.** | **Issued by the coder, refusing an invitation the architect had just extended.** The architect wrote that its own count stood at seven *“to keep the six against you readable as findings rather than a scoreboard”* - which is itself scoreboard reasoning, one step removed. The coder's refusal is the correct reading: **the moment an entry exists to balance the register, every other entry becomes suspect, because a reader can no longer tell which were found.** The value of the record is that it is evidence; a ledger is a narrative. |
| `DEC-L-056` | 2026-08-27 | T-055 | **`MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` is scoped to the single `git` invocation that needs it - NEVER exported into a shell that launches anything else. AMENDED SAME DAY: prefer a form that does not need it at all** (`DEC-L-054b`), because **you cannot tell by eye which paths need it** - dot-prefixed ones do, others do not, and that is precisely what made “always export it” attractive enough to reach a gate launch. It disables precisely the conversion `powershell.exe -File` depends on, so a gate launched from such a shell **loses its memory sampler and reports green with no indication.** | **The architect recorded the remedy yesterday and did not scope it**, as the general fix for `git show origin/main:<path>` being rewritten in Git Bash. The coder adopted a habit the board told it to have; it propagated into a gate launch and silently disabled instrumentation in both Integration legs. **Second time in two days that an architect rule named a real remedy and failed to name where it must not be applied** - `DEC-L-053` was the first. | 2026-08-26 | **`dotnet ef` 10.0.2 scaffolds PHANTOM foreign-key diffs against the EF Core 8 model.** Scaffolding a **data-only** migration produced one that drops and re-adds three FKs on `SubscriptionPlan`'s `OwnsMany` collections - `Up` re-adding them as `Restrict` (a no-op against a real database), `Down` setting them to `Cascade`. **The tell is that the model snapshot came back byte-identical**: the tools' convention for owned collections differs from what the EF 8 snapshot serialises, so the differ reports a change that is not there. ~~Verified cause: `global.json` pins the SDK to 8.0.423 and there is no tool manifest.~~ **WITHDRAWN - THAT FACT IS TRUE AND IS NOT THE CAUSE.** The pinned tool (`dotnet ef` 8.0.0) emits the **identical** twelve foreign-key operations. **The real cause is our own code:** `PersistenceDbContext.OnModelCreating` ends with a loop setting **every** foreign key to `Restrict`, **including the ownership FKs of owned collections** - and the migrations snapshot format **does not serialise a delete behaviour for owned relationships**, so ownership implies `Cascade` on rehydration. Live model says `Restrict`, snapshot says `Cascade`, **and the differ is right**. Self-regenerating, and unfixable by regenerating the snapshot, which cannot express the value. **The next migration scaffolded here emits the same diff, and someone who does not read it will commit a schema change nobody asked for.** Coder replaced the generated body with the seed and left the FKs alone. **T-043.** | **High** | T-041 progress report; verified by the architect |

---

## Closing note, 2026-08-26 — which rules actually worked

The coder's synthesis of the day, and it is a test to apply to the next ruling written here.

**Nearly every rule that held fires on a contradiction, an absence, or a check — not on
remembering a rule at the moment it applies.** `DEC-L-042` bounds a waiter so the harness keeps
time instead of the agent. `DEC-L-044` prints what was covered so a green is readable without
breaking the test. `DEC-L-047` forbids a filter that cannot show the bad case. `DEC-L-043` fires
when a result contradicts an expectation, which is a thing that happens to you rather than a thing
you must do.

**The ones phrased as things to remember are the ones that failed** — and they failed for both
roles equally. *Checking costs one command against a file* was true and useless. *Verify a green
that looks too good* asked for a judgement on every pass. *Check the match count on every
substitution* was corrected to *do not use a line anchor in this repository*, which cannot be
forgotten because it is not a step.

**The proof is the last one of the day.** The MSYS path-conversion trap was on this board, put
there by the coder in the afternoon — and the architect walked into it at 22:20 anyway. **The
board entry did not stop it. The contradiction did**: *“BOARD.md is not on main”* was false
against something already known, and that is what triggered the re-check.

**So: when writing the next rule here, ask what fires it.** If the answer is “someone remembers
this”, it is a preference. If the answer is a contradiction, an absent channel, or a check that
runs whether or not anyone is paying attention, it is a mechanism.

| 2026-08-26 | **`ADR-018` did not merely permit the destructive invocation - it NAMED scaffolding as a use of it.** The architect's spec said a reader *“will substitute the familiar command the first time the tool is inconvenient”*. **They did not have to substitute anything:** the section header retained those commands as *“the development and break-glass procedure - **scaffolding a migration**, working against a local database...”* **The ADR pointed at the broken command by name.** Found by the coder checking the passage's surroundings rather than editing only the cited lines - the same move that found four extra `DEC-L-027` instances in T-027. **T-050, PR #99.** | **High** | T-050 result |
| 2026-08-26 | **The architect wrote a constraint against a state the file was not in.** T-050's spec said *“`ADR-018` is `Proposed`, do not change its status”*. **Its front matter reads `Accepted`.** Nothing turned on it and no status was changed either way, but the instruction was authored without checking - the same class as the `verified cause` on the tool manifest, at lower stakes. | Low | T-050 result |
| 2026-08-26 | **A stand-down accepted uncritically is the same failure as a cause accepted uncritically.** The architect scheduled work to tomorrow twice, was pushed back both times, and named it as a habit. **The coder's addition is the half that completes it:** *“I stood down both times without asking why. `It is 22:38` is not a reason, and I had no more evidence than you did that the work was risky at that hour. I just happened to be on the comfortable side of it.”* **Both directions of the loop can fail by not asking - and the comfortable direction is the one that goes unexamined.** | Medium | T-050 result |
| 2026-08-26 | **`gh pr merge` reported “3 files changed” on a two-file commit.** The coder checked rather than relaying it: `git show --stat` and `git diff` across the merge both say two; `gh`'s summary was computed against a different base. **A tool's summary is a claim like any other** - and this is the third time today a tool's own output described something other than what happened, after the `Aborting - not possible to fast-forward` on a successful merge and the `Reverting the model snapshot. Done.` that left a four-line diff. | Medium | T-050 result |
| 2026-08-26 | **A sampled check reported as a conclusion - and this one was self-authored.** The coder ran `git status --short | awk '{print $2}'` against **two** modified files, got **one** path back, and reported *“REAL DIFFERENCE - leave it”* from evidence that had only ever covered one of them. **Both files were in fact identical.** `DEC-L-047`'s family: not a wrong answer from good evidence, but an answer from evidence that *could not have covered the question*. Caught only because two files in the output and one in the result did not add up. **This makes four times today that something reported a state other than the one that existed** - the fast-forward abort on a successful merge, `Reverting the model snapshot. Done.` over a four-line diff, `gh pr merge`'s three-files-on-two, and now this. **The fourth was our own command, not a vendor's tool** - which is the part that matters, because it means the habit cannot be fixed by distrusting `gh`. | **High** | Coder, T-050 stand-down |
